## Grand Prix Paris 2014

![](https://i.ormos.uk/28/18/281840a8a95798c2e0f21345dddd81b18df245dcb5acc332be359c2adfd973f7.jpg)  
  

It's been a splendid weekend here in Paris, France. It started out on Valentine's Day where 1,587 love hungry players went head to head for a spot in the second day of competition. Nine rounds later 186 of them could celebrate succeeding. Among the undefeated players Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa stood out as the hottest contender for the title. The Brazilian made it to the Top 8, but once there it was a 26-year-old **Magic Online** grinder who took the title home to Spain. Javier "Thalai" Dominguez made an excellent deck choice with his Blue-Black-Green Delver deck, opting to go without [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis), instead gunning to beat it.  
  
 With an 8-1 record after Day One he wrapped up the Swiss with 5-1 on Day Two before heading into the Top 8. There he took down fellow countryman Jóse Manuel Fernández Castelló before unleashing his discard spells and mana efficient creatures against Frenchmen Loïc Le Briand and Maxime Gilles.

With a Grand Prix in Spain in two weeks we might not have to wait long before seeing Javier Dominguez back in the spotlight. Even though he prefers Legacy it would be no surprise to see him experimenting with *Born of the Gods* in limited. That is, if he can find the time between playing Legacy on **Magic Online** against coverage reporters and Legacy experts alike.  
  
 Until next time, we say good bye and ... Congratulations to Javier Dominguez, champion of Grand Prix Paris 2013!

**Occupation:** Professional Legacy player  
  

**Previous Magic accomplishments:**  
 Various Legacy wins, Top 8s of small and big tournaments. I have been the main administrator for Legacy-France for six years. I innovated Omni-Tell with a friend and designed Counter-Grizzly. I'm also known as the best Doomsday player in the world.

**What deck did you play and why did you choose it?**  
 Imperial Painter for metagaming purposes. I had high expectations and still wanted to have an edge against very good players on Day 2. Painter is good versus what I expected and the predators thereof.

**What changes would you make to your deck and why would you make them?**  
 Nothing. If I wanted to change something I would talk to Jack Kitchen first. Thanks goes to him for the decklist and tips.

**What is the most important card in your deck?**  
 Altopiano, buy them while they are cheap!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/67/ac/67ac0c8b2d81fadd59e28817e52444d3d131a198b3364eec77251806eafb4c24.jpg)

 Deathblade. My friend didn't want to lend me his Sneak & Show and I had experience with Stoneblade.

## Quarterfinal Roundup

*by Olle Rade*

 

 

### José Manuel Fernández Castelló (BUG Delver) vs. Javier Dominguez (BUG Delver)

  
 The battle of the two Spaniards with the most aggressive decks in the top 8 were also the first match to finish. Their decks differed only a few cards from each other, the big difference being Fernández Castelló playing [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis), and Dominguez opting not to. Things were looking like that might be the deciding factor when Fernández Castelló took game one with a *Nemesis*. But Javier Dominquez clawed his way back into the match and won the next two games. With answers for [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) ranging from [Diabolic Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diabolic+Edict) to [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm) and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil).  
  
**Javier Dominguez 2-1 José Manuel Fernández Castelló**  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/15/34/15341eb80be1eb10e5f33e8a5aa948e80f5c93209d9093c07c84d69b22df2313.jpg)

*José Manuel Fernández Castelló vs. Javier Dominguez*

 

### Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa (UW Miracles) vs. Loic Le Briand (Reanimator)

Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa sat down for his quarterfinals facing the same opponent as in the last round of swiss. "I hope the game lasts longer than three turns this time," he commented, as Le Briand did indeed beat him in three turns in their last battle.  
 He got what he wished for. And except for the first game, where a turn two [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) put [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) into play the games were surprisingly long and interactive for a match between pure combo and pure control.  
 Unfortunately for the Brazilian the outcome was the same as in the swiss. Even though he had both [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) for a turn two [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) for Le Briand and a [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast) for the Frenchman's [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will). A [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) a few turns later once put another copy of the gigantic lifelinker into play.

**Loic Le Briand 2-1 Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6a/33/6a33d2b3659cf79f18408140341197fbea46d33269ef96665d78609abbf56016.jpg)

*Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa vs. Loic Le Briand*

 

### Maxime Gilles (UWR Miracles) vs. Stefan Böttcher (4-color Stoneblade)

When the king of the swiss continued his road to success in the quarterfinals he didn't really care what was on the other side of the table. A quick [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) locked up the first game, and 30 minutes later an [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels) sealed the deal. And in the second game Stefan Böttcher could only watch as numerous activations of [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) sculpted both the top of Gilles library and his hand into an unbeatable trump. [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels) once again dealing the final blow.  
  
**Maxime Gilles 2-0 Stefan Böttcher**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c1/2f/c12f31272479b4691c7c6d38bc55a6816c8ab395fc328060452a4d39a48ee49a.jpg)

*Maxime Gilles vs. Stefan Böttcher*

 

### Philipp Schönegger (UW Miracles) vs. Jean-Mary Accart (Imperial Painter)

  
 In a just as lopsided match on another table Philipp Schönegger had no problems assembling a [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) and [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) lock of his own. Jean-Mary Accart's combo deck based on [Blood Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Moon) and winning with [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant) and [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone) didn't live up to it's potential when he lost in two games that were only drawn out by the fact that both players were activating [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), and thinking through each turn very carefully.  
 "The matchup isn't so bad, but the matches tend to be very lopsided," he commented after the match.  
 "If I can get [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) in play it is very difficult to lose," Schönegger agreed.  
  
**Philipp Schönegger 2-0 Jean-Mary Accart** ![](https://i.ormos.uk/0c/37/0c379954f7f9feb4bdab4e7851fcd876ecd34082886f88ad20a1e3c663c189c6.jpg)

*Philipp Schönegger vs. Jean-Mary Accart*

* * *

## Sunday, 7:40 p.m. – Top 8 Disqualification

*by Tobi Henke*

 

There was quite a bit of excitement surrounding the announcement of the Top 8 at this Grand Prix when Gerardo Jurado Gibert, wo was poised to enter the playoffs with a score of 13-2, was instead disqualified from the tournament. Jurado Gibert first caught the attention of judges in round 13 when he committed what appeared to be, at the time, a simple game play error.

"The situation in round 13 involved a couple of play mistakes to his advantage, which could have been an honest mistake or a malicious attempt to gain an unfair advantage. At that time, though the situation looked suspicious, I didn't feel I had enough evidence to determine that the mistake was malicious," head judge Riccardo Tessitori explained. "After discussing it further with my colleagues, I realized that I had underestimated some details of the conversation at the table and I determined that the most appropriate solution was to exclude the player from the tournament."

Since the investigation was only concluded after the end of the Swiss rounds, Jurado Gibert was removed from the final standings. As is the usual procedure in such cases, the player who had been in ninth place before the disqualification was allowed to enter the Top 8 in his stead.

* * *

## Semifinal – Philipp Schönegger vs. Maxime Gilles

*by Tobi Henke*

 

"The mirror is pretty much the only match-up I've lost all weekend. To you and to Paulo," said Philipp Schönegger of Germany when the two players sat down for some Miracles versus Miracles business. France's Maxime Gilles certainly could feel happy about his chances here, as he had also won against Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa earlier in the day, in no small part thanks to his main-deck [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast) and [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/76/c5/76c53fa8d2f2096ff586dc4e20f9daec4eafc9d3dee5a53f5a43a92101673a00.jpg)

*Philipp Schönegger vs. Maxime Gilles*

 

### Game 1

The action started with [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) for Schönegger, forced past Gilles's [Counterspell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterspell), [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast), and [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage) with [Counterspell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterspell), [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce), and [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce). That exchange, however, left the way clear for Gilles to resolve [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) whereas, subsequently, Schönegger's [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) was stopped via [Counterspell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterspell).

A while later, Schönegger managed to resolve [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), but the opposing [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) had already amassed 11 counters. Two turns later, facing certain doom, Schönegger picked up his cards.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7d/f8/7df85e0804bf904468b551f6f968b783c3d9e6a2c554cf602c887e31a004ed13.jpg)

*Philipp Schönegger*

In this rather friendly match—Miracles players apparently stick together—the two players spent their time shuffling by making a game out of looking at each other's decklist and guessing what the other had sideboarded.

 

### Game 2

Both players began with [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) on turn one, but Gilles had the fearsome follow-up of [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) on turn two whereas Schönegger, well, didn't.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/3d/203d6eef704b097d6d04f7b8387b7c94b3c8baf02de835233ddd64939489a3ea.jpg)

*Maxime Gilles*

Unable to even develop his mana without the help of [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), Schönegger was falling further and further behind. The game continued for another couple of ages, but it could just as well have ended right there on the second turn. Schönegger never made a comeback.

**Philipp Schönegger 0-2 Maxime Gilles**

* * *

## Semifinal - Loïc Le Briand vs. Javier Dominguez

*by Tobi Henke*

 

Meeting in this semifinal were France's own Loïc Le Briand, playing Reanimator, and Spaniard Javier Dominguez, running BUG [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9a/cb/9acb282ce4bfd3c7105d47c34d2f6f0819d3e84a296bc7a5aa8ba0c1d1e7bbb0.jpg)

*Loïc Le Briand vs. Javier Dominguez*

 

### Game 1

Le Briand opened on land, [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal), Careful Stufy, and discarded a spare [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) plus [Ashen Rider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ashen+Rider). [Fear](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fear)ing [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze), he waited for turn two to cast [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate), though. A good choice, as Dominguez actually did have [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) in hand.

On turn three, Le Briand then forced the [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) with [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb) followed by [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume). Dominguez was caught between a rock and a hard place. He needed to stop [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) with [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) but returning a land was a problem too. He never could catch up to Le Briand's early lead.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e4/7f/e47fee1e058d1fc5635ff2c9952980242c50bc5e175e5ef809b399b104fa87b9.jpg)

*Loïc Le Briand*

 

### Game 2

In a complete reversal of the previous blow-out, Dominguez started with [Grafdigger's Cage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grafdigger%27s+Cage), [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), while Le Briand, after a mulligan to five, started slow. His [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) met [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), and even though he had [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) for Liliana, Dominguez's [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) eventually sealed the deal.

 

### Game 3

Another mulligan meant no fast action for Le Briand, while Dominguez had [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets). Le Briand cast [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) and [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) to set up a turn-four [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) for [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand), but Dominguez threw a wrench in the form of [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/02/cc/02cc30f97ad475edfb5d37506d2df9da657f2ff0fe12c31df80093816edd34cf.jpg)

*Javier Dominguez*

A second [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach) left Le Briand with exactly [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) as his only, lonely card, but it wasn't as if Dominguez needed such luck: he finished the game with two copies of [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) and three more blue cards in hand, in total control.

**Loïc Le Briand 1-2 Javier Dominguez**

* * *

## Finals – Maxime Gilles (FRA) vs. Javier Dominguez (SPA)

*by Olle Rade*

 

After a 15 rounds of swiss and both exciting quarter- and semifinals it was finally time for two players to get down to the deciding match for all the glory and the Grand Prix title. This weekend marked the first Grand Prix top 8 for both players at the table, where Javier Dominguez started the small talk by claiming he would be happy even with a loss. Looking at his opponents deck list prior to the match he raised an eyebrow.  
 "Three [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels), huh, so you haven't gotten a lot of draws I guess?"  
  
 Maxime Gilles could only smile and nod his head

  
 Gilles only loss this weekend was a concession to a fellow Frenchman in the last round of swiss, so technically he was still undefeated. But would his Blue White and Red Miracles deck be able to defeat the Spanish online grinder Javier "Thalai" Dominguez, piloting Blue Green Black [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets). In front of a crowd as passionate about Legacy as the rest of the French and Spanish **Magic** communities the finals started in a blazing fashion.  
  

### Game One

  
 Gilles had the perfect opener in game one, with both Divining Top and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), a combo he has been assembling all weekend. But Dominguez had other plans, discarding the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) with a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) and followed it up with [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach), netting a [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) for his efforts.  
  
[Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) from the top assembled Gilles semi lock, but Dominguez deck wasn't without answers. [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) countered a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) before Dominguez went for his trump – Liliana of the Veil. But Gilles looked at the top of his deck with the top and found ... ![](https://i.ormos.uk/61/cb/61cbf182f8c7be2b03b211d9a4c03b30ced88ccb7c253826692421d44cdf429d.jpg)

*Javier Dominguez*

  
 ... [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels). Countering the planeswalker.  
  
[Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) eventually took out the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), but Gilles had started his offense with a [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) and never needed to cast the [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels) waiting on the top of his deck.  
  

### Game Two

A [Null Rod](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Null+Rod) was the first permanent to hit the board in the second game, making clear Dominguez had a plan for how to defeat Gilles – by keeping him away from [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top).  
  
 He continued his initiative with a pair of [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), who did their best [Squire](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squire) impersonations and attacked for a few turns. A [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) joined the fun before Gilles dispatched a Shaman with [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) and the [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) with a [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast). Shaman number three entered and a hard cast [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus) met [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will). [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach) took out the remains of the French hand before the Deathrite damage brought the action to a decider.  
  

### Game Three

  
[Exhaustion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhaustion) started getting to the players shuffling for the final game as they shaked hands before dealing them their final seven cards. ![](https://i.ormos.uk/4a/16/4a161fed7b64da825c6c7dc44d3ed06486d83169a73de11bd0a6136bb69fb32c.jpg)

*Maxime Gilles*

No Sensei's Diving Top on turn one made Javier Dominguez let out a sigh of relief before he cast his best turn one play, [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets) who met a miracled [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus). [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach) followed, netting a [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) in response before discarding a [Rest in Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rest+in+Peace) and a land. A [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) was the card Gilles had hidden, but a second Hymn emptied his hand. [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) added to the injury and the Spaniard went on the offense, casting [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil) followed by a [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library), a card almost as old as the game itself, originally printed in *Legends* in 1994. The library fueled Dominguez hand with gas and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) followed.  
  
 Slowly, but surely, Maxime Gilles chances were looking slimmer and slimmer. He found a Swords for the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), but Liliana kept ticking up, and with an empty hand and only 4 lands in play would he be able to live for long?

Liliana's ultimate took out half of his lands and [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) joined for additional card drawing, proving to be the nail in the coffin for Gilles as Javier Dominguez claimed the match to the sounds of a loud cheer from his Spanish countrymen.

* * *

## Top 5 Cards

 

These were the cards that shaped the tournament, that sparked discussions and were the most debated, the cards that won games and turned Grand Prix Paris into an event to remember ...

 

![Thespian's Stage](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Thespian%27s+Stage)

 

### 5\. [Thespian's Stage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thespian%27s+Stage)

The land, in tandem with [Dark Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Depths), found its way into many decks of the format, some new, some old. The first big story of the weekend, for example, was Kasper Euser's 9-0 run with an innovative deck, dubbed "Farmville," which could assemble the combination quite quickly. It also made an appearance in various Lands decks or, thanks to [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), in Maverick.

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![True-Name Nemesis](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=True-Name+Nemesis)

 

### 4\. [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis)

Players have certainly adapted to the breakout card of the previous Legacy Grand Prix. [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm)s, [Diabolic Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diabolic+Edict)s, [Engineered Plague](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Plague)s, and even Marsh Casualities were everywhere, and the resurgence of Miracles as a deck may also be blamed to some extent on the fact that [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus) simply doesn't care about the 3/1's protection. As a result, no Nemeses made it to the Top 4. However, the need for players to run specific answers for [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) still warrants an inclusion in our Top 5 cards.

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Sensei's Divining Top](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top)

 

### 3\. [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top)

None other than Hall of Famer Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa called it the most important card in Miracles. Not only does it enable the discount on the deck's key spells [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus) and [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels), combined with fetchlands, over time, it makes for a massive increase in card quality. Furthermore, it allows players to beat decks heavy on discard and forms the second half of the feared [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)/Top combination.

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Deathrite Shaman](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Deathrite+Shaman)

 

### 2\. [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)

There was some debate among players which card was the best one-drop in the format. [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) doesn't yet have the numbers of the other big contender, but the Shaman has a lot going for it. Mana acceleration, graveyard removal, and a reasonable threat to the opponent's lieftotal make for an awesome package. It's not for nothing that the card has just been banned in Modern!

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Delver of Secrets](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Delver+of+Secrets)

 

### 1\. [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets)

[Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets), however, is still king of the format. Between Black-Blue-Green Delver, Red-Blue-Green Delver, and Blue-White-Red Delver, the card appeared in a fifth to a fourth of all day-two decklists. The BUG version with its ability to run [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Delver%2Bof%2BSecrets) as well as [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503075804/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) even went all the way. Eventual champion Javier Dominguez said he didn't want to choose between the two one-drops, and, fortunately, he didn't have to.
